#3355. Deutsche Bahn beginnt mit dem Verkauf des Familientickets

ICE im Hauptbahnhof Berlin: Reservierung bereits inklusive

Familien können für Fernverkehrsreisen mit der Deutschen Bahn ab sofort wieder das Familienticket buchen – und zwar dauerhaft. Das Angebot zum Preis von 99,99 Euro gilt für Reisen ab 1. November für eine Hin- und Rückfahrt im IC oder ICE, inklusive Sitzplatzreservierungen.

Die Bahn war Mitte 2025 heftig in die Kritik geraten, weil es die Familienreservierung abgeschafft hatte. Bis dahin konnten Familien für 10,40 Euro bis zu fünf Sitzplätze gemeinsam reservieren. Seitdem muss für jeden Platz einzeln bezahlt werden. In diesen Sommerferien bot das bundeseigene Unternehmen dann erstmals das Familienticket an – eine Art Antwort auf den Unmut über die abgeschaffte Familienreservierung. Aus dem temporären Angebot macht die Bahn nun ein dauerhaftes.

Beim Familienticket ist die Reservierung bereits inklusive. Mit dem Fahrschein können bis zu fünf Personen in den Fernverkehrszügen der Deutschen Bahn in der zweiten Klasse für knapp hundert Euro in ganz Deutschland hin- und zurückfahren. Eine einzelne Fahrt kostet 59,99 Euro.

Das Ticket gilt unabhängig vom Verwandtschaftsverhältnis. Ein Erwachsener (ab 15 Jahren) kann nach Bahn-Angaben mit bis zu vier Kindern oder zwei Erwachsene mit bis zu drei Kindern unterwegs sein.

Man werde das Familienticket »in einer sehr hohen Verfügbarkeit anbieten«, teilte eine Bahnsprecherin auf Anfrage mit. »Es gibt keine von vornherein beschränkten Stückzahlen.« Auf einzelnen, bereits stark gebuchten Verbindungen könne das Ticket aber auch einmal ausverkauft sein.

Der Sprecherin zufolge hat das Familienticket zu einer stärkeren Nutzung der Bahn durch Familien beigetragen: Im Sommer seien fast 100.000 Familien mehr als im Vorjahreszeitraum mit der Bahn gereist – mit Familienticket oder Sparpreisen.

Die Bahn beobachte seit Jahren eine steigende Nachfrage durch Familien. Daran habe auch die Abschaffung der Familienreservierung nichts geändert.

ICE im Hauptbahnhof Berlin: Reservierung bereits inklusive

#3356. 10 of the Best Witches’ Potions

It’s officially potion season! With Halloween around the corner, now is the perfect time to brew up spooky witches’ potions. Potions are a much loved activity in our house at any time of year, especially at Halloween! Take a look through this collection of fizzing, slimy, colour-changing, and even dry ice potions and let us know if you try any.

Potion making is a great activity just for fun, but it can also include lots of learning opportunities.

For younger children, add bowls, ladles, colanders and sieves for a brilliant, hands-on, sensory experience.

Fizzy potions are easy to make with baking soda and vinegar, and look amazing. I set up a fizzy witches’ lab for my daughter, and she loved it.

I used test tubes instead of larger containers, as it meant she could experiment easily with different colours and allowed her to make lots of eruptions with only a small amount of baking soda and vinegar.

Place a teaspoon of baking soda, a little water and a few drops of food colouring into each test tube.

Use the pipette to add vinegar to each tube. The mixture should fizz up out of the test tube.

TOP TIP – to make an extra foamy potion, add a little dish soap ( washing up liquid ) before adding the vinegar.

Experiment with different amounts of baking soda, washing-up liquid and vinegar to find the perfect fizz!

This stretchy slime has a great texture and is very easy to make. It can be squished, stretched and poured and feels deliciously gloopy and slimy!

Soak the chia seeds in water for a few hours.

When the chia seeds look thick and slimy, add a dollop of PVA glue and enough cornflour to make the mixture thick and gloopy. For a drier slime, add more cornflour.

If you’re concerned about children trying to eat the slime, you canmiss out the PVA glue. It just won’t stretch as well.

Colour changing potions using red cabbage indicator are a great first introduction to acids and alkalis. Children can experiment to find out how many different colours they can make!

Boil red cabbage in water for about 5 minutes. Allow the mixture to cool and save the liquid.

The red cabbage water is the indicator. It changes colour depending on whether an acid or an alkali is added.

Add a little indicator to each container and then add your test substance. The indicator should change colour.

I used vinegar, lemon juice and baking soda to create the colours below.

I really love the look of this steamy potion, on Fun Crafts Kids. However, remember to check out the safety warnings first.

We shared some easy potion ideas with Day Out With The Kids a while ago. Including creepy Goblin Snot Juice and colour-changing bat broth!

This potion is a green liquid with a layer of oil on top. If the container is shaken, the mixture separates into oil and water again! This is a great potion for learning about density.

The oil is less dense than the water, so it floats on the surface!

This witchy density jar is made in a similar way. Just pour coloured water into a jar with a layer of oil on the top, then try to find different creepy objects to float on the layers.

We found a bouncy ball eyeball floated on the top, and a coin sank to the bottom.

Print my FREE fizzy potion recipe and create your own potion with the blank recipe page.

The Imagination Tree has a beautiful Fairy Potion that you could turn into a Halloween potion.

We love the look of this polyjuice potion from This Grandma is Fun, too.

If you’re looking for an easy Halloween costume idea, Red Ted Art is full of inspiration!

Try our Halloween party science ideas, including fake blood, pumpkin lava lamps and spider decorations.

My book Gross Science, is jam packed full of gruesome, sticky and slimy science experiments for kids! Make jelly scabs, chocolate poo, slimy slugs, a blood model and lots more experiments, sure to creep out your friends and family!

I also have an exciting collection of Halloween STEM Challenges you might like. Including spider drawing robots, blowing up pumpkin balloons and more!

#3357. Reisen in die Boom-Region: Merz und Steinmeier gleichzeitig in Asien

Kanzler Merz besucht ab heute Singapur, Japan und Südkorea - und dürfte versuchen, Partnerschaften auszubauen, um die Abhängigkeit von China zu reduzieren. Dort wiederum ist Bundespräsident Steinmeier zu Gast

"Außenkanzler" Friedrich Merz stört es nicht sonderlich, wenn er so bezeichnet wird - zumeist von Kritikern. Eher im Gegenteil. Ein deutscher Bundeskanzler müsse sich um die Außenpolitik kümmern, betont Merz immer wieder und schiebt nach: "Außenpolitik, Außenwirtschaftspolitik und Wirtschaftspolitik gehen Hand in Hand." Deshalb spreche er auf seinen Auslandsreisen immer auch über die strategischen wirtschaftlichen Interessen Deutschlands.

Das gilt für seine fünftägige Reise in den Indopazifikraum ganz besonders: Singapur, Japan und Südkorea - alle drei Länder seien "für uns zentral, um kritische, einseitige Abhängigkeiten abzubauen", sagte der Kanzler, als er die Asien-Reise Mitte Oktober in seiner Regierungserklärung im Bundestag ankündigte. "Das ist gegenwärtig eine Schlüsselaufgabe unserer interessengeleiteten Außenpolitik und unserer Wirtschaftspolitik", unterstrich der CDU-Chef.

Chinas Exportoffensive trifft Europas Industrie. Warum höhere Zölle allein nicht ausreichen. mehr

Abhängigkeiten abbauen: Gemeint ist damit vor allem die Volksrepublik China. Der Gigant, der wirtschaftlich, technologisch, geopolitisch und militärisch alles in der Indopazifik-Region dominiert - mit Ambitionen weit darüber hinaus. Umso interessanter, dass Bundespräsident Frank-Walter Steinmeier parallel zur Reise des Kanzlers zum Staatsbesuch nach China fährt. Aus Regierungskreisen heißt es, der Besuch des Bundespräsidenten werde mutmaßlich stark dadurch geprägt sein, dass er zu einem Moment stattfinde, "zu dem Deutschland seine Chinapolitik neu austariert". Dazu sei in den vergangenen Wochen einiges entschieden worden.

In Deutschland und Europa gibt es große Sorgen wegen unfairer Wettbewerbsbedingungen im Handel mit dem Riesenreich. Hohe staatliche staatlichen Subventionen Pekings, Überkapazitäten in der Produktion und aggressive Preise schaden der deutschen Wirtschaft massiv - ebenso wie die von China künstlich niedrig gehaltene Landeswährung.

"Wir sehen nicht länger tatenlos zu", kündigte Friedrich Merz in seiner Regierungserklärung an, ohne die Volksrepublik namentlich zu nennen. "Wer mit uns den Handelskonflikt sucht, der wird spüren, dass wir uns jetzt zur Wehr setzen - und zwar mit angemessenen Reaktionen", betonte der Kanzler und verwies auf neue Handelsschutzinstrumente, die die EU-Kommission plant.

China akzeptiert EU-Handelsbeschränkungen für Hybridfahrzeuge. Doch wie nachhaltig ist die Einigung? mehr

Aus dem Bundespräsidialamt heißt es, Steinmeier wolle bei seinem Besuch über die problematischen Wirtschaftsbeziehungen sprechen, über Fairness und wie sich Handelskonflikte vermeiden lassen. Ein Treffen mit Staats- und Parteichef Xi Jinping ist für Freitag geplant. Dann dürfte es auch um den Krieg in der Ukraine gehen. Der Bundespräsident wolle darum werben, dass China seinen Einfluss auf Russland nutzt, um die Angriffe zu beenden und ernsthafte Verhandlungen über ein Ende des Krieges zu ermöglichen.

Der Ukraine-Krieg wird auch am Rande der Kanzler-Reise eine Rolle spielen, wenn Merz politische Gespräche mit den jeweiligen Regierungschefs von Singapur, Japan und Südkorea führt. Bei den diversen Terminen des Kanzlers geht es aber vor allem darum, die Partnerschaft mit diesen drei wichtigen Industrieländern auszubauen. "De-risking", Steigerung der Wettbewerbsfähigkeit und Innovation - darum solle es unter anderem gehen, heißt es in Regierungskreisen.

Die Bundesregierung wirbt auf einem Gipfel um internationale Geldgeber - und das ist auch dringend notwendig. mehr

"Wenn der Kanzler jetzt nach Singapur, Japan und Südkorea fährt, dann trifft er in das Herz unserer Ambitionen, mit diesen Industriepartnern mehr zu tun", sagt Wolfgang Niedermark, Mitglied der Hauptgeschäftsführung des Bundesverbandes der Deutschen Industrie (BDI). Japan ist nach China der zweitwichtigste Handelspartner Deutschlands in Asien, Südkorea liegt auf Platz drei. Der Insel- und Stadtstaat Singapur gilt als der zentrale Wirtschaftsknotenpunkt für Südostasien und den Indopazifik.

"Wir haben in den vergangenen Jahren vielleicht zu sehr auf den natürlich maßgeblichen Industriepartner China gesetzt", sagt der ausgewiesene Asien-Experte Niedermark. "Wir sehen jetzt aus verschiedensten Gründen, die hauptsächlich geopolitisch sind, aber auch im industriellen Wettbewerb mit China, dass wir das Risiko anders verteilen müssen."

Niedermark und andere BDI-Spitzenvertreter reisen in dieser Woche zunächst nach China und von dort weiter nach Seoul. In der südkoreanischen Hauptstadt findet vom 29. bis 31. Oktober die "Asien-Pazifik-Konferenz der Deutschen Wirtschaft" statt. Das hochrangige Treffen mit rund 1.000 Teilnehmern aus Politik, Wirtschaft und Wissenschaft wird am Donnerstag von Merz eröffnet.

Wirtschaftsministerin Katherina Reiche (CDU) übernimmt zusammen mit Siemens-Vorstandschef Roland Busch die Leitung der Konferenz. "Es ist gut und wichtig, diese Beziehungen zu pflegen", betont der BDI-Repräsentant Niedermark und verweist auf die hochrangige Wirtschaftsdelegation, die den Bundeskanzler in der Regierungsmaschine nach Asien begleitet. An Bord sind unter anderem Vertreter aus den Bereichen der Industrie- und Technologieunternehmen, der Verteidigung und der Energie. Die Namen von Teilnehmern gibt die Bundesregierung aber gewöhnlich bis zum Abflug nicht bekannt.

Niedermark findet es "klasse", dass Merz und Steinmeier gleichzeitig in die boomende Region Asien-Indopazifik reisen. Nötig sei ein Engagement auf verschiedenen Spuren: "Wir müssen mit China weiter im Gespräch bleiben. Wir müssen aber gleichzeitig neue Allianzen mit etablierten und neuen Partnern in der Region Asien suchen."

Dieses Thema im Programm: tagesschau.de | Partnerschaften stärken: Merz reist für fünf Tage nach Asien | 26.10.2026 |

#3358. In Overfished Adriatic Sea, Dolphins Look to Trawlers for Food

Bottlenose dolphins follow a trawler off the coast of Veneto, Italy. Dolphin Biology and Conservation

Off the eastern coast of Italy, large numbers of bottlenose dolphins are looking to fishing trawlers as a source of food, a sign that dolphins may be struggling to feed themselves in waters depleted by overfishing.

Dolphins in the Adriatic Sea are most drawn to otter trawlers, which drag a large conical net near the ocean floor, according to the study, published in Frontiers in Mammal Science. Dolphins trailed 26 percent of otter trawlers near Veneto and 76 percent near Marche, eating discarded fish or darting into moving nets to gather food.

“We estimated that the bottlenose dolphin populations of Veneto and Marche, combined, exceed 1,000 individuals,” said coauthor Silvia Bonizzoni, head of the group Dolphin Biology and Conservation. “Between 86 and 90 percent of the dolphins, depending on region, were photographed one or more times while they were following trawlers.”

On average, dolphins followed 41 percent of otter trawlers and 35 percent of midwater trawlers, which also drag conical nets but operate closer to the surface. That compares to previous research from the Adriatic, carried out in the 1990s, which found that dolphins were trailing just 10 percent of trawlers. Dolphins have, it seems, grown more reliant on trawlers for food despite the many hazards involved.

“It is known that bottlenose dolphins are occasionally injured or killed by trawl gear,” said coauthor Randall Reeves, of the U.S. Marine Mammal Commission. “Dolphins may also suffer hearing damage that results from chronic exposure to the noise of trawlers. It is risky behavior. However, finding sufficient prey away from trawlers in an overfished sea may be too difficult. It appears that for these animals, taking the risks is better than going hungry.”

Supertrawlers Are Taking Antarctic Krill That Whales Depend On

#3359. Geleceğin Türk hukukçuları uluslararası mahkemelerde tarihi duruşmaları izledi

Sanat, edebiyat ve iş dünyasının buluşma noktası olan, kamuoyunda "Kahve" adıyla bilinen Rindan ekibinin hamiliğinde hayata geçirilen projede, yazılı sınav ve mülakatlarla seçilen öğrenciler Strazburg, Lüksemburg, Paris ve Lahey'de, 1-10 Eylül'de saha çalışması yürüttü.

Toplam 40 kişilik öğrenci ve akademisyen heyeti, 12 haftalık teorik eğitimin ardından Avrupa'nın en önemli uluslararası yargı mercilerini ziyaret ederek tarihi nitelikteki duruşmaları yerinde izledi.

Avrupa İnsan Hakları Mahkemesi (AİHM), Avrupa Birliği (AB) Adalet Divanı, Uluslararası Adalet Divanı (UAD), Uluslararası Ceza Mahkemesi (UCM) ve Milletlerarası Ticaret Odası (ICC) gibi kurumları ziyaret eden öğrenciler çalışmaları yakından inceledi.

"Uluslararası Hukuk Yolculuğu Projesi"nin danışmanı avukat Kamil Uğur Yaralı, AA muhabirine yaptığı açıklamada, projenin temellerinin bürosunda gençlerle yürüttükleri hukuk ve edebiyat sohbetleri sırasında atıldığını söyledi.

Yaralı, öğrencileri, öğrendikleri teorik dersleri sahada uygulama imkanı bulmaları için AİHM, UAD, AB Adalet Divanı, UCM ile Uluslararası Tahkim gibi önemli yargı kurumlarına götürdüklerini belirterek, "Öğrenciler duruşmalara girdiler, yetkililerden brifingler aldılar. Özellikle teorik derslerin verimli geçtiğini, duruşmalarda ve temaslarda yönelttikleri sorulardan gayet iyi anlamış olduk." diye konuştu.

Öğrencilere seçim sürecinde İngilizce sınav ve mülakatların yapıldığını dile getiren Yaralı, interaktif derslerin yanında felsefe, mantık ve hukuki argümantasyon becerilerini artırmak adına kitap tahlilleri ve film kritikleri gibi disiplinler arası etkinliklere de yer verdiklerini anlattı.

Türk hukukçularının uluslararası alandaki görünürlüğünü artırmayı temel hedef olarak belirlediklerine dikkati çeken Yaralı, "Ana amacımız ve vizyonumuz, Türk hukukçuların uluslararası görünürlüğünü ve temsilini artırmak, nitelikli temsile katkıda bulunmak." dedi.

Yaralı, projenin edebiyat kahveleri geleneğinden gelen "Rindan" ekibi, Gençlik ve Spor Bakanlığı, Türk İşbirliği ve Koordinasyon Ajansı (TİKA) ile Diyanet İşleri Başkanlığının desteğiyle hayata geçirildiğini, çalışmayı kurumsal bir yapıya kavuşturarak diğer üniversitelerin katılımıyla sürdürmek istediklerini belirtti.

Projenin genel koordinatörü Boğaziçi Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi Dr. Öğr. Üyesi Hasan Basri Bülbül, teorik eğitimin ardından öğrencilerin yargı mekanizmalarını bire bir tecrübe etmelerini hedeflediklerini söyledi.

Ziyaret takvimini özellikle UAD'de görülen kritik bir davaya göre ayarladıklarını belirten Bülbül, şöyle devam etti:

"Uluslararası Adalet Divanı bizim için oldukça önemliydi. Almanya’nın Filistin’de yaşanan soykırıma destek verdiği gerekçesiyle Nikaragua’nın açmış olduğu davanın ilk itirazlarının incelendiği duruşmaya öğrencilerimiz katılma imkanı buldu. Ancak mahkeme, 'Dinleyici locasına ilk gelen girer' kuralıyla dünyadan yalnızca 15 kişi kabul ediyordu. Rezervasyon imkanı yoktu. Öğrencilerimiz saat 10.00'daki duruşmaya girebilmek için sabah 05.00'te kalkıp mahkeme kapısında sıra bekledi. 3-4 gün boyunca peyderpey bu şekilde içeri girerek davayı takip ettiler."

Bülbül, Hollanda'nın Lahey kentinde bulunan UCM'de Sudan İç Savaşı'nda işlendiği öne sürülen suçlara ilişkin yargılamayı da yerinde takip ettiklerini aktararak, "Sadece mahkemelerin binalarını ziyaret edip kuru bilgiler almayı amaçlamadık. Aynı zamanda bu mahkemelerde yaşanan süreçlerin öğrenciler tarafından gerçekten tecrübe edilmesini istedik." dedi.

Kosova'nın eski Cumhurbaşkanı Haşim Taçi'nin yargılandığı mühim davanın son duruşmalarını da takip ettiklerini anlatan Bülbül, "Öğrenciler de bu noktada uluslararası yargı mekanizmalarının, uluslararası adalet sisteminin nasıl işlediğine dair bir fikir edinme imkanı buldu." diye konuştu.

İstanbul Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi öğrencisi Ahmet Doruk Akbaş, projenin Türkiye'nin uluslararası kurumlardaki temsilini artırma hedefine hizmet ettiğini söyledi.

Akbaş, "Projeyle Türkiye'nin uluslararası yargı organlarında, uluslararası savunma mekanizmalarında ve uluslararası örgütlerdeki varlığına hizmet edebilecek, buralarda bir kariyer inşa etmemizi sağlayacak çok iyi fırsatlar, eğitimler ve deneyimler elde etmiş olduk." ifadelerini kullandı.

Boğaziçi Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi öğrencisi Yuşa Özölçer, 12 haftalık eğitimde ders aldıkları Jose Argüello Gomez'i Uluslararası Adalet Divanı'nda Nikaragua adına savunma yaparken görmenin son derece etkileyici olduğunu dile getirdi.

Özölçer, yurt dışında Türkiye'den mezun olan hukukçuların temsil oranlarının çok düşük olduğunu belirterek, "Bunları yerinde görerek kendimize ilham almak, ileride bizim de bu konumlara gelebileceğimize dair inancımızı artırdı." dedi.

Marmara Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi öğrencisi Mehmet Eren Kozalı, eğitimin interaktif yapısına dikkati çekerek, "Uzak görülen o uluslararası kurumların hiç de öyle olmadığının farkına vardık. Süreç bizim için ulaşılabilir oldu. Divanda tarafların yürüttükleri savunma biçimleri çok öğreticiydi." diye konuştu.

İstanbul Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi mezunu olan, Londra Üniversitesi'nde uluslararası hukuk alanında yüksek lisans eğitimini sürdüren Nurefşan Akbaş ise projenin temellerinin atıldığı 2021 yılındaki ilk katılımcılardan biri olduğunu belirtti.

Akbaş, "Bu gezi bizim için bir hayal gibiydi, yıllar sonra tamamlanması beni çok mutlu etti. Derslerde gördüğümüz mahkemeleri bizzat ziyaret etmek, özellikle Nikaragua/Almanya duruşmasına katılmak uluslararası hukuk alanında ilerlemek isteyen biri olarak çok değerliydi." ifadelerini kullandı.

İstanbul Üniversitesi Hukuk Fakültesi'nde yüksek lisans eğitimini sürdüren Muhammed Yusuf Kaba da "Sadece hukuki bir gezi değil, kültür ve sanat anlamında da dolu dolu bir süreçti. Şehirler ve kurumlar üzerine otobüs yolculuklarında birbirimize sunumlar yaptık. Son derece doyurucu bir etkinlik oldu." değerlendirmesini yaptı.

Anadolu Ajansı web sitesinde, AA Haber Akış Sistemi (HAS) üzerinden abonelere sunulan haberler, özetlenerek yayımlanmaktadır.Abonelik için lütfen iletişime geçiniz.

Bu haberde bir hata mı var? Her türlü talep, şikâyet ve görüş için okur temsilcimize ulaşabilirsiniz.

#3362. Hofreiter verlangt Einsatz der Marine gegen russische Schattenflotte

Schwedische Einsatzkräfte auf dem Weg zu einem bereits geenterten Tanker der russischen Schattenflotte (im März dieses Jahres)

Für den Grünenpolitiker Anton Hofreiter gehören die Schiffe der russischen Schattenflotte konsequent gestoppt. Der Vorsitzende des Europa-Ausschusses im Bundestag fordert den Einsatz der Bundeswehr gegen derartige Schiffe, die durch deutsche Seegebiete fahren. »Die Schiffe sind illegal unterwegs, sie brechen die Gesetze. Deshalb können die Schiffe gestoppt werden, [...] im Idealfall von der deutschen Marine«, sagte Hofreiter weiter.

Anton Hofreiter (Bündnis 90/Die Grünen), Vorsitzender des Europa-Ausschusses im Bundestag

Andere Länder wie Frankreich oder Belgien hätten solche Schiffe bereits »gebordet«, sagte Hofreiter im Podcast »Berlin Playbook« von »Politico« . »Deutschland, obwohl eine Reihe Schiffe durch deutsche Seegebiete fahren, hat noch kein einziges wirklich gebordet in den vergangenen Monaten«, kritisierte der Grünenpolitiker. »Und deshalb: Nein, Deutschland tut nicht genug.«

Seit mehreren Jahren versucht der Kreml, mit der Schattenflotte einen von westlichen Unterstützern der Ukraine eingeführten Preisdeckel für russisches Öl zu umgehen. Dafür werden meist veraltete Schiffe mit schwer nachvollziehbaren Eigentumsverhältnissen gechartert und verschiedene Methoden genutzt, um die Herkunft von Ölladungen zu verschleiern.

Falls die Bundespolizei für entsprechende Einsätze nicht ausreichend ausgestattet sei, müsse das Innenministerium Amtshilfe bei der Bundeswehr anfordern. »Wenn der politische Wille da wäre, könnte man sofort handeln«, sagte Hofreiter.

Notwendig wäre aus seiner Sicht ein Beschluss des Bundestags. Das würde die Wahrscheinlichkeit erhöhen, »dass die Regierung sich traut«. Es könne auch eine Form von EU-Mandat sein, das sei aber völkerrechtlich nicht einfach.

In maroden Tankern schippert Russland einen großen Teil seiner Ölexporte an der deutschen Küste entlang. Eine Anfrage der Grünen, die dem SPIEGEL exklusiv vorliegt, offenbart massive Wissenslücken der Behörden. Mehr zu Methoden der Tankermafia lesen Sie hier .

Schwedische Einsatzkräfte auf dem Weg zu einem bereits geenterten Tanker der russischen Schattenflotte (im März dieses Jahres)

Anton Hofreiter (Bündnis 90/Die Grünen), Vorsitzender des Europa-Ausschusses im Bundestag

#3364. Dollarregen für deutsche Wertarbeit: US-Investoren auf Einkaufstour

Mehr Deals mit Deutschland: Immer mehr Geld US-amerikanischer Investoren fließt in traditionelle deutsche Unternehmen wie Birkenstock. Die schwierige Suche nach Firmennachfolgern begünstigt den Übernahmetrend.

Was haben die deutschen Traditionsunternehmen Birkenstock, Viessmann, Linde und EBM-Papst gemeinsam? Kurze Antwort: Sie alle wurden von US-amerikanischen Investoren aufgekauft.

Traditionelle deutsche Familienbetriebe und mittelständische Unternehmen scheinen auf US-Unternehmen und Investoren eine besondere Anziehungskraft auszuüben. Nach einer Auswertung des Finanzdatenanbieters Pitchbookgab es im vergangenen Jahr insgesamt 76 Übernahmen und Beteiligungen an deutschen Industrie- und Fertigungsunternehmen mit US-Beteiligung.

Insgesamt summierten sich den Angaben zufolge die Transaktionen 2025 auf rund 29,5 Milliarden US-Dollar. Das ist fast doppelt so viel wie im Jahr 2022. Bis zum 19. August dieses Jahres kamen bereits 23,9 Milliarden US-Dollar hinzu.

"Das Interesse von US-Investoren ist definitiv weiter vorhanden", erklärt Samar-Ahmad Albrecht-Mahmood von Veracity Capital Investmentsaus Frankfurt am Main auf DW-Anfrage. Die Firma berät Unternehmen, die in Deutschland investieren wollen.

Deutsche Unternehmen seien insbesondere im Industrie- und Fertigungssektor attraktiv, "weil viele von ihnen hoch spezialisiert sind, starke Kundenbeziehungen haben und sehr gutes technisches Know-how besitzen."

Außerdem erreichten viele deutsche Unternehmer ein Alter, in dem sie über den Verkauf ihres Unternehmens nachdenken. "Da es oft keinen Nachfolger innerhalb der Familie gibt, kommen Unternehmen auf den Markt, die vor ein paar Jahren vielleicht nicht zum Verkauf gestanden hätten", so Albrecht-Mahmood.

Prominente Übernahmebeispiele in diesem Jahr waren der Mulfinger Ventilatoren Spezialist Ebm-Papst, für den das US-Unternehmen Madison Air im August 5,4 Milliarden Dollar zahlte.

Außerdem wurde im Juni 2026 das Augsburger Traditionsunternehmen Everllence, vormals MAN Energy Solutions, an Bain Capital verkauft. Die US-amerikanische Investmentgesellschaft erwarb 51 Prozent der Anteile.

Nach Angaben des Branchendienstes Die deutsche Wirtschaftbesitzt kein Land mehr deutsche Unternehmen als die USA. Von den insgesamt 7.861 ermittelten Unternehmen in der Datenbank "Deutsche Unternehmen in Auslandsbesitz" befinden sich 1.750 in US-Besitz.

Auch die Wirtschaftsprüfer von PricewaterhouseCoopers (PwC) sehen die "Destination Deutschland" im Trend. Laut gleichnamigen Reporthaben zwar nicht die Anzahl der Übernahmen und Beteiligungen zugenommen, wohl aber das finanzielle Volumen (siehe Grafik).

Der Bericht hebt ausdrücklich die massiven Investitionen in Höhe von 7,7 Milliarden Euro der US-Investmentgesellschaft Carlyle, in Partnerschaft mit der Qatar Investment Authority (QIA) beim Kauf des Unternehmens BASF Coatings hervor. Der Deal mit der traditionellen Firma für Fahrzeuglacke und Oberflächentechnik wurde am 26. Oktober 2025 vereinbart und am 30. Juni dieses Jahres vollzogen.

Laut einer aktuellen Umfrage der amerikanischen Handelskammerin Deutschland planen die meisten der befragten US-Unternehmen, ihre Aktivitäten in Deutschland auszuweiten. 62 Prozent gingen davon aus, dass sich die Bedingungen in den nächsten drei bis vier Jahren verbessern werden.

Mehr als die Hälfte (52 Prozent) der befragten US-Firmen gaben an, ihre Aktivitäten in Deutschland in diesem Zeitraum ausbauen zu wollen, ein leichter Anstieg gegenüber 49 Prozent im Jahr 2024.

Die Zunahme von US-Firmenübernahmen- und Beteiligungen scheint allerdings auf den ersten Blick nicht zu den rückläufigen US-Auslandsinvestitionen in Deutschland im vergangenen Jahr zu passen. Denn laut Bundesbankfloss 2025 deutlich weniger Investitionskapital aus den USA nach Deutschland als im Jahr zuvor.

Zwar sind die Auslandsinvestitionen in Deutschland insgesamt gestiegen, von 57,4 Milliarden Euro im Jahr 2024 auf insgesamt 86,1 Milliarden Euro im vergangenen Jahr. Doch aus den USA kamen nur noch 11,8 Milliarden Euro, knapp 44 Prozent weniger als 2024. Dagegen stiegen die Investitionen aus Großbritannien auf rund 26 Milliarden Euro.

Experte Albrecht-Mahmood versucht, den Widerspruch aufzulösen. "Der Trend setzt sich fort, auch wenn das nicht einfach bedeutet, dass es jedes Jahr mehr Deals gibt", erklärt er. Das allgemeine Interesse an guten deutschen Industrieunternehmen, besonders an starken Mittelständlern, bleibe hoch.

Ein weiterer Punkt, so Albrecht-Mahmood, sei die Bewertung. "Im Vergleich zu den USA sehen wir immer noch Situationen, in denen deutsche Unternehmen moderater bewertet werden, besonders im KMU-Segment (KMU = kleine und mittelständische Unternehmen - Anm. d. Red.). Das kann Übernahmen für internationale Käufer ziemlich attraktiv machen."

#3365. The Impossible Loop – Make a Double Möbius Strip

A Möbius strip is a loop with one side and one edge. It’s made by twisting a strip of paper 180 degrees and taping the ends together. There’s no obvious difference between inside and outside. If you draw a line around the centre of the paper, it’ll cross over to the other side, and you’ll end up back where you started.

This is a great fun, inexpensive science challenge that will have you and your friend scratching your heads with wonder!

Cut two strips of paper about 30cm long and 2 cm wide, although the size isn’t too important.

Take one end of the strip and twist it once. Tape the ends together. This is a Möbius strip.

Cut down the middle of the strip to form a magic loop. This is a loop with two twists!

This time, twist the strip twice before taping the ends together.

Cut down the middle of the strip to create two linked loops of paper.

The Möbius strip was discovered in 1858 by two German mathematicians independently of each other.

A Möbius loop is commonly used in the image for recycling, indicating that an item can be recycled!

Learn about gravity and air resistance with paper spinners.

Design and build a paper aeroplane launcher.

Learn about strong shapes with paper towers.

#3366. A Home Battery Revolution Is Reshaping the Power Grid

A technician installs a Base Power home battery system that will sell electricity to the power grid in Texas. Base Power

As residential batteries have become more energy dense, cheaper, and smaller, more households are storing their excess solar power. Now, utilities and energy companies in dozens of countries are buying up those electrons, bundling them together, and using them to balance the grid.

Home and commercial solar arrays provide nearly a fifth of Australia’s electricity generation, with panels atop one in every three homes. To extend those panels’ usefulness, owners are increasingly buying home batteries not only to store their power for later use, but to sell electrons to the grid at times of high demand. The arrangement enables grid operators to more effectively manage the mismatch between midday solar generation and real-time consumer demand, a process known as balancing. It also lowers market energy prices because utilities that draw on batteries can avoid building expensive new power plants and power lines.

Australia laid the groundwork for this transformation last year by offering homeowners and small businesses a 30 percent discount on residential batteries, which resulted in 430,000 battery installations in less than a year, three times more than expected. A recent expansion of the Cheaper Home Batteries Program is expected to boost the number of installations to more than 2 million by 2030. If they agree to install a smart meter, battery owners can sell energy to the grid and put cash in their pockets: between $80 and $1,600 a year, depending on how the program is structured.

In a dozen other countries, mostly in Europe and North America, grid operators are writing checks to homeowners for the right to lease their batteries. “We’re moving toward a world where homes don’t just consume energy — they store it, optimize it, and contribute back to the grid,” says Joe Frodsham of the Texas-based energy storage manufacturer Renon Power. A critical mass of home batteries scattered across a region and networked together through so-called virtual power plants, or VPPs, he says, marks “the shift from energy storage as backup to energy storage as an active grid asset.”

Unlike a net metering system, which sends unused energy from rooftop solar panels directly into the grid in return for an energy credit, a VPP requires a storage system and software that tells the battery to send energy to the grid when it needs more power, like on a hot summer day. Compensation for tapping a homeowner’s battery is paid by either a local utility or a VPP program, of which there are now more than 500 in the U.S. and thousands in Europe.

This rapid expansion of home batteries and advanced software that aggregates thousands of decentralized energy sources is “transforming not only the way electricity is generated, but also how it is traded, delivered, and consumed,” concludes a 2022 International Energy Agency report. These assets, the report said, “can provide valuable services to the grid when incentivized with appropriate technologies, policies, and regulations.”

Currently, fewer than 10 percent of Australian homeowners who have solar arrays have signed contracts with energy providers. But experts believe the model has immense potential to expand, thanks to a global “battery revolution” that has, in a matter of years, seen battery prices plummet and their storage capacity shoot up even as their size has shrunk. Today, a 10 kilowatt-hour unit — which can simultaneously run a few household appliances and some lighting and electronics for 24 hours — can snugly fit under a staircase or into a garage corner. Between 2010 and 2020, battery density increased by more than 700 percent, and between 2010 and 2023, the price of lithium-ion batteries plunged from about $1,400 per kilowatt-hour to less than $140 per kilowatt-hour — one of the fastest cost declines of any energy technology in history.

The Amber Electric smartphone app allows homeowners in Australia to manage their battery systems. Courtesy of New South Wales Climate and Energy Action

Climate experts hope that grids can be cheaply and effectively balanced by hundreds of thousands of batteries distributed across cities, suburbs, and rural areas — some in electric vehicles, others on the walls of garages or cellars, and some in utility-scale storage parks, which still provide the lion’s share of solar-energy storage everywhere in the world. Ideally, aggregating the capacity of decentralized batteries — whether they are charged by solar panels or directly through the grid during off-peak hours — will replace dirty gas peaker plants.

Large battery projects, says a May report from the energy think tank Ember, “are increasingly cost-competitive and faster to build than new gas power plants.” And their carbon footprint is about 87 percent smaller than an average-size gas peaker. Home batteries offer similar advantages. When home battery systems are programmed to charge during times of high renewable output and discharge during peak grid demand, studies show they can reduce average household emissions by 2.2 to 6.4 percent.

A first among major nations, India is industrializing with solar. Read more.

Last year, the amount of U.S. home battery capacity enlisted in virtual power plants grew by 153 percent. Programs in Puerto Rico and California that paid homeowners for their stored energy were a “key driver of the growth,” according to policy and research analyst Madeline Turner of San Diego-based Ohm Analytics. California’s VPP program, according to Canary Media, “has shown that its fleet of home batteries can be relied on much like a traditional power plant.” During a two-hour test last July, roughly 100,000 home batteries delivered about 539 megawatts of energy — more than the output of a large gas peaker plant.

In the U.S., an installed 10 kilowatt-hour system costs roughly $8,000 to $13,000. A 30-percent federal clean energy credit ended in 2025, although customers can still benefit until 2027 from tax incentives by leasing a battery system from a commercial solar or battery company. California offers an additional baseline rebate of around $150 per kilowatt-hour.

Residential storage markets function differently from country to country, and in the U.S. from state to state, as do their payment schemes. In Germany this spring, Octopus Energy’s PowerDrive bundle began providing customers with a smart meter and an EV charger that enables electricity to flow in two directions, allowing it to manage its customers’ EV charging in exchange for up to 10,000 free miles of driving, plus an annual bonus of up to $409 if the EV is plugged in, at home, for 300 or more hours. Octopus makes money selling the power stored in customers’ EVs when demand peaks and prices spike. The nation’s EV ownership rate is just under 3 percent, though, so the total impact of vehicle-to-grid technology is quite small.

Since 2022, the U.K. has had a system that pays homeowners for reducing demand when the grid is stressed — whether by high demand or a lack of wind, which provides about 30 percent of the U.K.’s total electricity generation. Battery owners have the advantage of being able to rely on their batteries during these periods. In Puerto Rico, which has a particularly rickety power grid, some 70,000 home batteries are helping to reduce the risk of blackouts, according to the grid operator.

Germany’s largest VPP is Statkraft, whose software links a multitude of decentralized energy resources including a few large fossil-fueled power plants, biogas and hydroelectric plants, thousands of solar and wind farms, and thousands more residential and commercial batteries. It markets its tidy bundles of energy on short-term European power exchanges.

Home batteries have become the biggest source of battery capacity in Germany. Figures reflect the total battery capacity in January of each year. Source: Bundesnetzagentur. Yale Environment 360 / Made with Flourish

With the growing demand for power, and long waits for grid connections, utilities are prepared to pay storage owners for the right to lease their batteries. But because the demand for and price of energy on a macro scale is different than the needs of a single household, most VPPs won’t optimize price fluctuations to benefit a household budget. Rather, they will optimize those fluctuations to benefit their own business model. A homeowner may prefer to charge their battery overnight, when the price of power drops, and discharge it in the late afternoon, when prices surge. But a VPP will charge and discharge the battery as needed to balance the grid — even if prices are unfavorable to the homeowner.

The primary drawbacks of joining a VPP, says Toby Couture of E3 Analytics, a Berlin-based energy think tank, are the household’s loss of control over when and how much power a third party can call upon (though most plans allow battery owners to set a reserve level), uncertain financial returns, and some additional wear and tear on the battery from extra cycling. A 2025 study found that EVs enrolled in a VPP program degraded 9 to 14 percent faster over a 10-year period. Another drawback is the high purchase price of home batteries, although some countries and several U.S. states offer subsidies.

As it boosts renewables, China still can’t break its coal addiction. Read more.

Australia’s policies, which have reduced regulatory hurdles and challenges to integrating residential power, have made it the frontrunner in bidirectional storage, and similar policies in other countries could propel the clean energy transition forward. Where two-way battery storage makes financial sense to grid operators and battery owners, whether large or small, virtual power plants will likely expand in places where regulatory conditions allow, experts say. This is the logic of a battery revolution that is just beginning to transform our electricity markets.

Paul Hockenos is a Berlin-based writer whose work has appeared in the The Nation, Foreign Policy, New York Times, Chronicle of Higher Education, The Atlantic, and elsewhere. He has authored several books on European affairs, most recently Berlin Calling: A Story of Anarchy, Music, the Wall and the Birth of the New Berlin. He was a fellow at the American Academy in Berlin. More about Paul Hockenos →

#3370. Massenproteste gegen Rundfunkreform in Tschechien

Die tschechische Regierung will den öffentlich-rechtlichen Rundfunk künftig aus dem Staatshaushalt finanzieren - und Mittel für die Sender deutlich kürzen. Landesweit protestierten Zehntausende. Sie befürchten weniger Unabhängigkeit.

Dicht gedrängt stehen die Demonstrierenden am Sonntagnachmittag trotz wechselhaftem Wetter auf dem Altstädter Ring im Prager Stadtzentrum. Zehntausende sind dem Aufruf der Initiative "Eine Million Augenblicke für Demokratie" gefolgt.

Sie demonstrieren gegen die Pläne der Regierung, die Gebühr für den öffentlich-rechtlichen Rundfunk abzuschaffen und Fernsehen und Radio stattdessen direkt aus dem Staatshaushalt zu finanzieren. Das Budget der öffentlich-rechtlichen Medien würde damit um mehr als 15 Prozent gekürzt werden.

"Hände weg von den Medien" ist auf Plakaten zu lesen oder auch "Heute Geld, morgen Inhalt". Kritiker fürchten, dass durch eine Finanzierung aus dem Staatshaushalt die Unabhängigkeit der Berichterstattung gefährdet ist.

Die 18-jährige Abiturientin Sara ist mit einer Freundin zur Demo gekommen. "Wir sind hier, weil wir finden, dass die Medien unabhängig sein sollten und nicht durch das bedroht werden dürfen, was ein Politiker denkt. Und wir finden, dass die Art, wie die Regierung mit diesem wichtigen Gesetz umgeht, unangemessen ist."

Den Plan, den Rundfunk ab Januar 2027 aus dem Staatshaushalt zu finanzieren, hatte die rechtspopulistische Regierung schon im Sommer verkündet.

Am Freitagabend hatte die Koalition dann aber kurzfristig und für viele Beobachter überraschend den Gesetzesentwurf ins Parlament eingebracht und darüber in einer ungewöhnlichen Nachtsitzung abstimmen lassen, um kurz nach halb fünf am Samstagmorgen.

Schon am Freitagabend fand deshalb spontan eine Demonstration vor dem Parlament statt. In einigen anderen Städten Tschechiens wurde am Wochenende ebenfalls protestiert.

Daran, wie die Parlamentssitzung abgelaufen ist, stört sich auch Demonstrant Zdenek Skala auf dem Altstädter Ring. "Wir finden, dass über eine solche Änderung ausführlich und öffentlich debattiert werden müsste. Das wird aber nicht getan - und offensichtlich soll diese Debatte absichtlich verhindert werden."

Neben Plakaten, tschechischen Flaggen und auch EU-Flaggen sind auf dem Altstädter Ring am Sonntag auch viele Menschen mit Hüten aus Zeitungspapier zu sehen. Eine Anspielung auf die Fernsehfigur Večerníček, das tschechische Äquivalent zum Sandmännchen.

Auch Plakate mit der tschechischen Zeichentrickfigur des kleinen Maulwurfs, der weint, ragen aus der Menschenmenge. Lenka Bartakova ist mit ihrem Kind zur Demonstration gekommen. Den Kinderwagen hat sie mit bekannten Fernsehfiguren aus Plüsch dekoriert.

"Ich habe das unter dem Kommunismus erlebt, deshalb möchte ich auf keinen Fall, dass so etwas zurückkommt", sagt Lenka Bartakova. "Sie sagen zwar, es gehe nur um die Gebühren und sie würden keinen inhaltlichen Einfluss nehmen - aber das werden sie tun. Sobald die Politiker das in der Hand haben, weiß niemand, was danach passiert."

Premierminister Andrej Babiš widerspricht: Niemand habe die Absicht, in die Berichterstattung einzugreifen. Die Finanzierung des Rundfunks würde durch die Gesetzesänderung stabiler und vorhersehbarer, teilte Babiš auf X mit.

Machtkampf in Tschechien: Wer darf zum NATO-Gipfel - der Ministerpräsident oder auch der Präsident? mehr

Besorgt äußerte sich hingegen der liberale Präsident Petr Pavel. Er kritisierte, ebenfalls auf X, die Art und Weise, wie das Gesetz zur Abstimmung gebracht worden ist. Diese untergrabe den "Glauben an die guten Absichten der Initiatoren", so Pavel.

Die Politikwissenschaftlerin Vladimira Dvorakova vermutet in der kurzfristig anberaumten Parlamentsabstimmung auch politisches Kalkül. Bei den Senatswahlen Anfang Oktober hatte die Opposition deutlich besser abgeschnitten als die Regierungsparteien.

"Es ist der Versuch, die Aufmerksamkeit auf etwas anderes zu lenken und einen Kulturkampf statt einer Debatte zu führen", sagt Dvorakova. "Einen anderen Grund dafür, warum es so schnell ging, gibt es eigentlich nicht."

Bislang hat die Abschaffung der Rundfunkgebühren nur die erste Hürde genommen, sie könnte aber trotzdem bereits Anfang des nächsten Jahres in Kraft treten.

Für den 3. November haben die Beschäftigten des öffentlich-rechtlichen Rundfunks zum Streik aufgerufen. Und für Mitte November ist bereits die nächste große Demonstration angekündigt.

Dieses Thema im Programm: tagesschau.de | Tschechien - Protest gegen Rundfunkreform | 25.10.2026 | 21:55 Uhr

#3373. After a Civil Rights Complaint, Chicago Built Largest Air Monitoring Network in the U.S.

This article was originally published by Grist and is reproduced here as part of the Climate Desk collaboration.

A jogger runs along the shoreline of Lake Michigan, with heavy smoke from the Canadian wildfires in the background, in June 2023. Kamil Krzaczynski / AFP via Getty Images via Grist

As extreme heat worsens pollution, a network of 277 monitors will identify pollution hot spots.

Serap Erdal stopped at a light pole in Chicago’s Grant Park, pulled out her phone, and began pinching at the screen. Behind her, towering skyscrapers cut into a sunny blue sky as she scanned her palm-sized map of the city. The researcher barely noticed the hum of city buses, cars, and cyclists buzzing around her in the city’s busy downtown. She was working out what was in the cool summer air.

Fixed to the pole above her was one of the city’s new solar-powered air quality monitors. The tracker, encased in a metallic silver shell about the size of a tissue box, is part of the nation’s largest community air quality monitoring network. Today, the network has 277 air monitors across Chicago collecting air pollution data from every ward and community area, with an increased concentration in already-overburdened neighborhoods.

A bright green dot flashed on Erdal’s phone. She smiled.

“Currently, the air quality index at this location is 31,” said Erdal, a professor of environment and occupational health sciences at the University of Illinois Chicago. The reading puts the air quality in the city’s public park in the Environmental Protection Agency’s safest category, meaning it poses little to no risk to public health. “Because we have a clear day and it’s breezy, concentrations across the city are quite uniform,” she added.

On that day in June, almost all of the city’s monitors were green, except for one on the far South Side, where legacy industrial facilities and freight traffic pump emissions into nearby Black and Latino neighborhoods. In the coming years, the monitoring system is expected to elucidate the dramatically uneven air quality in different neighborhoods, even on clear and breezy days.

The project, called Open Air Chicago, went live last fall and is part of a five-year project to collect hyperlocal air quality data and provide Chicagoans with real-time pollution information. The data is also intended to help officials develop guidance for permitting, urban planning, and air quality control. The network is about to face its first Chicago summer, when air pollution typically worsens. Pollution from cars, heavy vehicles, and industry reacts with sunlight and heat, forming ground-level ozone, a harmful pollutant and the key ingredient in smog, in the summer. As climate change makes summers longer and hotter, the conditions for smog formation are also becoming more common.

The monitoring effort originated as a result of a fight over the city’s decision to relocate General Iron’s scrap-metal shredding operation from the mostly white Lincoln Park neighborhood to the predominantly Latino and Black Southeast Side. In 2021, local environmental activists filed a civil rights complaint with the federal Department of Housing and Urban Development, arguing that the move discriminated against low-income communities of color and harmed their health.

The city and the community groups reached a settlement in 2023, which included launching the community air monitoring network. Chicago officials partnered with the University of Illinois Chicago to launch it last fall at a combined cost of over $4 million to cover operations through the beginning of 2030.

“This air monitoring system is creating an ongoing record of what the air quality is in Chicago,” said Oscar Sanchez, the director of the Southeast Environmental Task Force, one of the groups that successfully filed the civil rights complaint. South and West Side residents have so far had limited ways to corroborate that their air is unsafe. Although they tend to have higher rates of respiratory issues, they lacked time-stamped data to establish connections between their poor health and the region’s air quality. Sanchez said the monitoring system changes that.

“This is Chicago working in good faith,” he said. “We’re here to ensure that there’s publicly available information so people are not gaslit about their experience.”

Each air monitor is less than a mile from the next. The low-cost equipment measures ground concentrations of two airborne pollutants: nitrogen dioxide, typically formed by the combustion of fossil fuels, and PM2.5, which are small particles just one-twentieth the width of a single human hair and capable of passing through a person’s respiratory system and entering the bloodstream. Exposure to both pollutants is linked to childhood asthma and cardiovascular issues. PM2.5 is increasingly being singled out as the world’s leading environmental health-determining factor — associated with acute mortality and morbidity for respiratory and cardiovascular health outcomes.

Even as air quality has improved in recent decades, it can still reach unhealthy levels during the summer when sunlight and warm temperatures react with pollutants in the air to form ground-level ozone. That seasonal smog can further degrade air quality when it mixes with smoke from increasingly frequent wildfires. Climate change is exacerbating these conditions in the Midwest, according to Daniel Horton, an assistant professor of Earth and planetary sciences at Northwestern University.

“We also have to deal with the consequences of increased frequency and intensity of wildfires,” Horton said. “That’s a problem that doesn’t necessarily occur in our backyards, but when the wind blows in the right direction, we suffer the consequences in the Midwest.”

Wildfire smoke now dependably turns Chicago’s summer skies into a hazy orange. In 2023, smoke from the record-setting Canadian wildfires reached the Windy City and raised ground-level ozone levels by nearly 10 percent of the federal pollution limit, according to a study published earlier this year. The study also found that central, western, and southeastern neighborhoods in Chicago were most impacted by ozone.

So far, wildfires have already burned through 2.5 million acres nationwide. That’s nearly double the 10-year average for this time of year. The recent surge in wildfires, tied in part to climate change, is reversing the country’s steady progress toward improving air quality, according to a recent study published earlier this month in Science.

Between 2003 and 2015, the study found that stricter federal air quality rules successfully cut down on the toxic gases that form ozone, or smog, by approximately 11 percent. Since 2015, however, rising ozone levels have undone about a third of the nation’s headway toward cleaner air — translating to an increase of 318 premature deaths per year from wildfire-related ozone since 2013.

As extreme heat reshapes air pollution in the Midwest and across the country, Horton said the Open Air Chicago network will be able to identify localized pollution hot spots, offering an “unprecedented look at the air quality landscape across the city.”

The local network is expected to build on top of existing data collected by NASA satellites and the EPA’s limited number of regulatory-grade air monitoring sensors, which Horton called the “gold standard.” While the EPA’s more sophisticated monitoring devices provide precise measurements of pollutants like PM2.5 and nitrogen dioxide, they are also more expensive to maintain. Chicago’s low-cost sensors, although less precise, capture over 20,000 data points per day. The sheer volume of data is expected to yield major findings about how air quality changes across the city.

Carl Malings, an assistant research scientist at Morgan State University and NASA Goddard Space Flight Center, said the local data is especially important because, in many cases, satellites are capturing an aerial view that combines the messy interplay of particles and gases throughout the atmosphere’s layers, making it difficult to untangle what’s in the air people breathe near the surface of the Earth.

“If you see a smoke plume from satellite data in the absence of other information, it could be hard to say, Is that smoke actually reaching down to the surface, where it’s impacting air quality and people’s health, or is it rising a little bit above the surface and passing overhead?” Malings said.

Back in downtown Chicago, Erdal said the program is expected to run through 2029. City officials hope to keep the network online even longer. Over the noisy downtown traffic, she said the network is the culmination of two decades of citizen-based research with communities across the city’s West Side and Southeast Side. Previously, she worked on a project to monitor vehicle emissions in several of the city’s majority-Latino neighborhoods, which included helping local environmental justice activists install low-cost PurpleAir sensors.

Now her big goal is that the data collected over the next five years can help craft a roadmap for city officials and community leaders to cut down Chicagoans’ exposure to unsafe air.

“We hope we’ll strengthen the network in the future,” Erdal said. “Measuring more pollutants and providing more data to the public.”

—Juanpablo Ramirez-Franco, Grist and Chicago Public Media

#3375. Persistent neuronal stress signalling underlies Angelman syndrome phenotypes

Thank you for visiting nature.com. You are using a browser version with limited support for CSS. To obtain the best experience, we recommend you use a more up to date browser (or turn off compatibility mode in Internet Explorer). In the meantime, to ensure continued support, we are displaying the site without styles and JavaScript.

Nature (2026) Cite this article

Angelman syndrome is a severe neurodevelopmental disorder caused by the loss of UBE3A, a gene that encodes an E3 ubiquitin ligase1,2. The mechanisms that underlie Angelman syndrome are not well understood, and in particular, the link between UBE3A substrates and disease phenotypes remains uncertain3. Here, using gain- and loss-of-function mutations in UBE3A4,5 along with unbiased proteomics, we show that asparagine synthetase (ASNS) is a target of UBE3A. We find that ASNS protein abundance is increased in the brains of Ube3a-null mice and in human neurons6. Genetic correction of ASNS overabundance rescues microcephaly and many behavioural deficits. Detailed molecular analyses show that ASNS overabundance activates stress signalling and causes a global reduction in neuronal protein synthesis. Our results suggest that loss of UBE3A traps neurons in a state of persistent stress and stunts neuronal growth during developmentally sensitive periods. Moreover, our results indicate that targeting ASNS and the stress response could hold therapeutic potential in Angelman syndrome.

Angelman syndrome (AS) occurs in 1:20,000 births and is characterized by intellectual disability, developmental delay, epilepsy, microcephaly, dysmorphic facial features and a unique behavioural profile1,2. It is caused by deletion or mutation of the maternally inherited copy of UBE3A, a gene also linked to mood disorders7,8, autism4,5,7 and cancer9,10. It is widely accepted that phenotypes in AS arise due to the dysregulation of UBE3A substrates during development. However, although numerous substrates have been proposed3, establishing their link to AS phenotypes has proved difficult, limiting researchers’ mechanistic understanding of this disorder.

UBE3A is the founding member of the HECT (homologous to E6-AP C terminus) domain family of E3 ubiquitin ligases, all of which have a conserved HECT domain that contains the necessary biochemical machinery to accept activated ubiquitin from E2 enzymes and to transfer it to target proteins9. UBE3A expression is imprinted specifically in neurons such that only the maternally inherited allele is expressed, with the paternal allele being epigenetically silenced11. This imprinting mechanism, along with many disease-related phenotypes, is conserved in AS model mice that lack a maternal copy of Ube3a (Ube3am−/p+)12. These phenotypes include motor and behavioural deficits, and microcephaly, the most prominent neuroanatomical deficit in AS13. Microcephaly is not present at birth in mice but is acquired postnatally, driven by a failure of neuronal growth that results in shorter and simplified dendritic arbors and smaller-diameter axons14,15.

The integrated stress response (ISR) is a conserved pathway that helps maintain cellular homeostasis in response to noxious stimuli, including proteotoxic stress, nutrient deprivation, viral infection and oxidative stress16. These stimuli are sensed by four specialized kinases (PERK, GCN2, PKR and HRI) that converge on phosphorylation of the α-subunit of eukaryotic translation initiation factor 2 (p-eIF2α) to suppress global protein synthesis and initiate compensatory transcription programs. Asparagine synthetase (ASNS) is a conserved metabolic enzyme that is induced by the ATF4 transcription factor in response to amino acid deprivation17. ASNS catalyses the ATP-dependent synthesis of asparagine and glutamate from aspartate and glutamine, but its role in neuronal development remains poorly understood.

To identify substrates of UBE3A, we used cell lines expressing gain-of-function (GOF) and loss-of-function (LOF) mutants4,5 and subjected them to stable isotype labelling by amino acids in cell culture (SILAC), tryptic remnant immunoaffinity enrichment (di-Gly purification) and mass spectrometry18 (Fig. 1a). We performed this experiment in quadruplicate and identified proteins whose ubiquitination levels were enriched by at least 20% in cells expressing a hyperactivating mutation in UBE3A (Supplementary Table 1). Because of technical issues in this experiment (see Methods for details), the total numbers of detected proteins varied across replicates. However, comparing unique proteins across replicates allowed us to compile a list of 23 proteins that could serve as potential substrates (Fig. 1b). Within this list were novel substrates, as well as many proteins that are known to bind to or become ubiquitinated by UBE3A (Fig. 1c).

a, Schematic of the SILAC workflow in HEK293T cells expressing LOF or GOF mutants of UBE3A. b, Venn diagram showing the number of identified proteins in liquid chromatography–tandem mass spectrometry (LC–MS/MS) analysis. A comparison of experiments performed in quadruplicate identified 23 common substrates (n = 4 biological replicates). c, List of putative UBE3A substrates and their gene names identified in our screen. Green indicates known UBE3A substrates or interacting proteins; bold indicates substrates confirmed by secondary validation. d,e, Western blot (d) and quantification (e) of candidate proteins from HEK293T cells transfected with WT MYC-UBE3A or MYC-UBE3A(LD). Data are mean ± s.e.m. Significance determined by two-sided one-way analysis of variance (ANOVA) with Tukey’s test for multiple comparisons. Exact P values are shown. f, Venn diagram showing the overlap of putative substrates identified in this study with proteins that co-immunoprecipitate with UBE3A in HEK293T cells18. g, Venn diagram showing proposed UBE3A substrates among upregulated proteins identified by mass spectrometry from two lines of AS neurons derived from induced pluripotent stem cells. Full protein profiles are shown in Supplementary Table 2. All sample sizes (n) represent independent biological replicates: n = 4 in b, n = 3 (ASNS) and n = 4 (DNA-PKc) in e.

To validate these candidates, first we compared protein abundance by western blot analysis in HEK293T cells expressing (1) wild-type (WT) or ligase-dead UBE3A (C820A; UBE3A(LD)), and (2) WT UBE3A or the GOF UBE3A(T485A) mutant5. In both experiments, the abundance of the catalytic subunit of DNA-dependent protein kinase (DNA-PKc), ASNS and the ribosomal protein RPL3 was affected by UBE3A in a manner consistent with these proteins being substrates of UBE3A (Fig. 1d,e and Extended Data Fig. 1a–d). Second, we generated knockdown (KD) cells stably expressing a short hairpin RNA (shRNA) targeting UBE3A (UBE3A KD), and analysed candidate substrate abundance. A moderate reduction of UBE3A (25%) increased the abundance of ASNS, DNA-PKc and RPL3 in these cells (Extended Data Fig. 1e,f). Third, we treated cells with the proteasome inhibitor MG132 (15 μM, 12 h) to test proteasome dependence. MG132 reversed the effect of WT UBE3A on DNA-PKc and ASNS abundance but had no effect on RPL3, suggesting that the UBE3A-elicited changes in RPL3 were proteasome independent (Extended Data Fig. 1g,h).

We next compared the results of our screen with the findings of a previous co-immunoprecipitation experiment using UBE3A(LD) overexpressed in HEK293T cells18. Fifteen of the 23 candidates, including ASNS and DNA-PKc, were also identified as binding partners in this previous experiment (Fig. 1f). Finally, we compared a list of 55 proposed UBE3A substrates, including ASNS and DNA-PKc, with a previously reported dataset of upregulated proteins identified in two independent patient-derived AS neuron lines6 (Supplementary Table 2). Of the 532 proteins that were commonly upregulated in both lines, only 4 proposed substrates were on this list, including annexin A119, peroxiredoxin 120, PEG106 and ASNS (Fig. 1g). Because every validation step pointed to ASNS, we expanded our analysis of this protein.

ASNS is composed of a glutamine hydrolysing domain and an asparagine synthase domain, reflecting its two catalytic activities (Extended Data Fig. 2a). We first performed co-immunoprecipitation experiments and found that overexpressed UBE3A co-purifies with endogenous ASNS in HEK293T cells, consistent with our previous mass-spectrometry results18 (Extended Data Fig. 2b). We next reconstituted an in vitro ubiquitin reaction consisting of recombinant human ASNS, UBE1 (E1), UBCH7 (E2), UBE3A, ubiquitin and ATP. We found that UBE3A was required to catalyse the time-dependent formation of high-molecular-weight ubiquitin chains on ASNS while simultaneously reducing the level of unmodified ASNS (Extended Data Fig. 2c–e). Finally, we examined ASNS ubiquitination in UBE3A-KD cells expressing HA-tagged ASNS and Flag-tagged ubiquitin and treated with MG132 (30 μM) for 1 h. A modest decrease in UBE3A produced a proportional 25% decrease in ASNS ubiquitination (Extended Data Fig. 2f,g), suggesting that UBE3A is the principal E3 enzyme targeting ASNS for proteasomal degradation in these cells.

Our proteomics experiment identified five candidate ubiquitination sites on ASNS: K83 and K168, which are poorly conserved and located in the glutamine hydrolysing domain; and K467, K478 and K504, which are more highly conserved and are found in the ASNS domain (Extended Data Fig. 2h). To confirm UBE3A-dependent ubiquitination at these sites, we generated ASNS lysine-to-arginine (KR) mutants and co-transfected them with Flag-tagged ubiquitin and UBE3A into HEK293T cells. Whereas ubiquitination of the K83R and K168R mutants resembled what was observed with WT ASNS, it was reduced or altered for the K467R, K478R and K504R mutants (Extended Data Fig. 2i). In steady-state experiments, WT UBE3A reduced the abundance of WT ASNS and the K83R and K168R mutants, but no changes were observed for the K467R, K478R and K504R mutants (Extended Data Fig. 2j,k). Because degradative ubiquitination is rarely directed by a single lysine, we cannot rule out changes in cooperativity, ubiquitination linkage or ubiquitination kinetics arising from K467R, K478R and K504R mutations. Nonetheless, these results strongly suggest that ubiquitination in the asparagine synthase domain is crucial for ASNS degradation.

We next assessed UBE3A-mediated ASNS regulation in vivo by analysing ASNS protein levels and ubiquitination in the brains of Ube3am−/p+ mice (referred to as Angelman mice). In hippocampal tissue, immunoprecipitated ASNS showed a reduction in ubiquitination of about 50% in Angelman mice, compared with WT controls (Extended Data Fig. 3a,b). Similarly, ASNS abundance was increased in the hippocampus (130.63 ± 3.92% above WT) and cortex (114.55 ± 3.60%) with a trend in the midbrain, but not in cerebellar samples (Extended Data Fig. 3c). These changes were not due to an increase in Asns transcription, because quantitative PCR (qPCR) of hippocampal RNA showed that there was no difference in Asns mRNA levels (Extended Data Fig. 3d). Finally, ASNS protein levels in hippocampal lysates from mice with an equivalent maternal GOF T485A mutation in UBE3A (mT503A in mice21) showed a subtle but consistent decrease (Extended Data Fig. 3e,f). Altogether, our biochemical experiments show that UBE3A ubiquitinates ASNS both in vitro and in vivo.

ASNS is expressed widely across tissues and cell types21; however, its expression in the brain is not well characterized. We visualized Asns transcripts in mouse glutamatergic neurons, GABAergic neurons and non-neuronal cells using whole-brain single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) and spatial transcriptomic data22. Notably, Asns was expressed broadly in both excitatory and inhibitory neurons but was mostly absent in non-neuronal cells (Fig. 2a). When we extended this analysis to cell subclasses across the brain, we found that Asns was expressed in most neuronal subclasses but was undetectable in nearly all non-neuronal cells (Fig. 2b and Supplementary Table 3). We further confirmed this using a published scRNA-seq dataset from the mouse hippocampus23, which again showed that Asns expression was enriched in neurons (Fig. 2c), a pattern that matches the neuron-specific imprinting of Ube3a.

To characterize ASNS protein levels in situ, we validated an antibody against mouse ASNS protein using mouse Neuro2a cells transfected with a plasmid that expresses EGFP along with an shRNA that targets mouse Asns. We confirmed the specificity of the antibody through immunofluorescence experiments (Extended Data Fig. 4a–d). We first examined ASNS protein in the developing brain, focusing on the cortex and hippocampus. In mice at postnatal day 0 (P0), ASNS staining was most prominent in the cortical neuronal layers. Cell bodies and apical dendrites were prominently labelled, whereas axonal fibres showed subtle labelling, and staining was mostly absent from proliferative zones adjacent to the lateral ventricles (Fig. 2d). By P10, ASNS immunoreactivity was diffuse throughout the cortex and hippocampus, with more prominent somatic labelling in deeper-layer cortical neurons and the pyramidal layers of the developing hippocampus. By P21, ASNS staining was intermittent throughout the brain and enriched in deep layers of the cortex, the dentate gyrus, neuronal layers of the hippocampus, the amygdala, and thalamus (Fig. 2d,e). In other regions, ASNS strongly labelled most serotonin (5-hydroxytryptamine; 5-HT)-positive serotonergic neurons of the dorsal raphe (88.20 ± 5.77% of cells) and a subset of tyrosine hydroxylase (TH)-positive dopaminergic neurons in the substantia nigra pars compacta (62.36 ± 3.83% of cells), suggesting that ASNS protein is heterogeneously maintained across the brain (Fig. 2f,g).

To test whether these ASNS-enriched cells were neurons, we co-stained for markers of neurons (NeuN), astrocytes (GFAP) and microglia (IBA1) in the cortex and hippocampus. Virtually all ASNS-positive cells were co-localized with NeuN, confirming that ASNS transcript and protein are predominantly neuronal (Fig. 2h–j). We next asked whether the heterogeneous labelling was stochastic or systematic by examining ASNS in inhibitory cells of the stratum oriens and stratum radiatum. If stochastic, ASNS-enriched cells should be represented equally across inhibitory neuron subclasses. If systematic, then ASNS enrichment should be biased towards specific subclasses. We co-stained brain slices for ASNS and several widely used markers of interneurons, including parvalbumin (PV), somatostatin (SST), calretinin (CR) and neuropeptide Y (NPY) (Extended Data Fig. 4e–g). Of note, all ASNS-positive cells were NPY-positive (PV, 56.94 ± 6.91%; SST, 57.5 ± 3.82%; CR, 22.62 ± 12.43%; NPY, 100%), suggesting that ASNS enrichment is not stochastic but maintained by functionally distinct classes of cells.

Finally, we asked whether loss of UBE3A increases ASNS protein abundance in selected neuronal populations. Using immunofluorescence, we examined ASNS abundance in NYP-positive interneurons and CaMKIIα-positive excitatory neurons in the hippocampus (Extended Data Fig. 4h–k). We saw a subtle increase in in both excitatory and inhibitory cells, which suggests that the loss of UBE3A affects ASNS abundance broadly rather than in specific cell populations.

We next wondered whether correcting ASNS overabundance could affect aberrant phenotypes in Angelman mice. So far, only one Asns hypomorphic mouse line has been published24. We therefore generated a new null line that contains a CRISPR–Cas9-targeted nonsense mutation in Asns. We crossed heterozygous Asns-null males (Asns+/−) with paternal-null Ube3a females (Ube3am+/p−) to generate four genotypes: WT (Ube3am+/p+, Asns+/+), Het (Ube3am+/p+, Asns+/−), Angelman (Ube3am−/p+, Asns+/+) and Rescue (Ube3am−/p+, Asns+/−) (Fig. 3a). As expected, western blotting and immunostaining analysis showed that Het mice had roughly 50% of WT ASNS protein levels, and ASNS abundance in Rescue mice returned to near WT levels (Fig. 3b and Extended Data Fig. 5a–c).

For behavioural assays (e–i), sample sizes are as follows: n = 71 total in e,f (WT: 5 males, 11 females; Angelman: 5 males, 8 females; Het: 12 males, 7 females; Rescue: 12 males, 11 females); n = 138 total in g (WT: 12 males, 20 females; Angelman: 15 males, 11 females; Het: 16 males, 25 females; Rescue: 22 males, 17 females); n = 138 total in h,i (WT: 12 males, 21 females; Angelman: 15 males, 11 females; Het: 16 males, 24 females; Rescue: 22 males, 17 females).

Behavioural phenotypes in Angelman mice are well characterized12 and we used a range of assays to examine these established phenotypes (Fig. 3c). Angelman mice are known to exhibit adult-onset obesity, which becomes apparent around 3 months of age14. We observed a similar obesity phenotype that was greatly improved in Rescue mice, independent of sex (Fig. 3d and Extended Data Fig. 5d). In the open field assay, Angelman mice ambulated less and spent more time in the centre of the arena than control mice did25. Notably, Rescue mice were indistinguishable from WT mice in terms of time spent in the centre of the arena (Fig. 3e), even though they showed deficits in the total distance travelled (Fig. 3f). We found additional phenotypic improvements in Rescue mice in the hindlimb clasping assay (Fig. 3g) and the marble bury assay (Fig. 3h,i). Notably, Het mice performed indistinguishably from WT mice in every assay.

Reducing the overabundance of ASNS did not rescue all phenotypes. For example, Rescue mice showed no improvement in the rotarod assay (Extended Data Fig. 5e). Angelman mice are known to skip a period of sleep, known as the siesta, that normally occurs during a mouse’s active phase26. We observed a similar phenotype in our Angelman mice, but sleep measures did not improve in Rescue mice (Extended Data Fig. 5f–h). These results indicate that many—but not all—AS phenotypes in mice result from the dysregulation of ASNS, suggesting that there are additional disease-relevant substrates.

Given that ASNS maintains amino acid homeostasis, we investigated the effects of UBE3A loss on the levels of amino acids in the brains of Angelman mice. We isolated hippocampi from all four genotypes at P21 for metabolomic profiling (Fig. 4a). As expected, levels of asparagine were decreased in Het and Rescue mice, both of which lack one copy of Asns (Fig. 4b and Supplementary Table 4). Conversely, asparagine levels were increased in Angelman mice, as compared with WT controls, functionally confirming the overabundance of ASNS in these mice. Of note, no amino acid was decreased, but rather, we observed significantly increased levels of phenylalanine, glycine and proline, as well as a trend towards increased levels of glutamine, histidine, leucine and tyrosine (Fig. 4b). All of these changes returned to baseline in Rescue mice, suggesting that they resulted from ASNS overabundance.

Cysteine was not detected in our samples. c, Western blot and quantification of p-eIF2α abundance (normalized to total eIF2α) from P21 brain lysates. d–i, RNA-seq analysis from P21 cortex. d, Volcano plots showing DEGs in Angelman mice relative to WT controls (FDR < 0.05). FC, fold change. e, Venn diagram of overlapping DEGs (edgeR; FDR < 0.05). f,g, GO enrichment analyses showing downregulated (f) and upregulated (g) DEGs. h,i, Heat maps representing DEGs associated with oxidative phosphorylation (GO: 0006119) (h) and cytoplasmic translation (GO: 0002181) (i). For quantitative panels, data are mean ± s.e.m. relative to controls. Statistical significance determined by two-sided one-way ANOVA with Tukey’s multiple-comparison test in b and by two-sided two-way ANOVA with Tukey’s multiple-comparison test in c. Statistical significance for the GO enrichment analysis (f,g) was determined using the hypergeometric test, with multiple testing correction applied via the Benjamini–Hochberg FDR method using ShinyGO (v0.85.1). Exact P values are indicated. All sample sizes (n) represent independent biological replicates (mice): in b, n = 12 per genotype (6 males and 6 females); in c, n = 3 (WT, Het and Rescue) and n = 4 (Angelman) from three independent experiments, with two Angelman samples analysed in a single experimental run; in d–i, n = 6 per genotype (WT: 3 males and 3 females; Angelman: 4 males and 2 females; Het: 3 males and 3 females; Rescue: 3 males and 3 females).

Loss of amino acid homeostasis is closely linked to the ISR, a conserved pathway that ultimately halts protein synthesis. Activation of the ISR is typically detected through increased phosphorylation of the eIF2α translation initiation factor at serine 51 (p-eIF2α)27. To determine whether this pathway was engaged in Angelman mice, we assessed the levels of p-eIF2α by western blot in brain hemisphere lysates. We observed a significant increase in p-eIF2α in Angelman mice (176.43 ± 7.13% of WT), indicating increased ISR signalling (Fig. 4c). Notably, this increase was improved by correcting ASNS overabundance in Rescue mice (p-eIF2α; 140.56 ± 10.94% of WT).

We next asked whether simple overexpression of ASNS is sufficient to activate the ISR in neurons. We used an adeno-associated virus (AAV) encoding EGFP or ASNS-T2A-EGFP (in which a 2A self-cleaving peptide (T2A) is inserted at the 5′ end of Asns) in cultured mouse cortical neurons, and assessed their effect on ISR signalling. Overexpression of ASNS was sufficient to increase the levels of p-eIF2α above EGFP-alone controls (129.27 ± 2.20% of control) (Extended Data Fig. 6a,b), indicating that ASNS overabundance alone is sufficient to activate the ISR.

Amino acid starvation activates a branch of the ISR known as the amino acid response (AAR). Because our metabolomic studies showed that free amino acid concentrations were altered in Angelman mice, we performed RNA-seq to determine whether the AAR is genuinely engaged in these mice. We sequenced RNA extracted from the cortex and hippocampus of P21 WT, Angelman and Rescue mice. We found few differentially expressed genes (DEGs) in the hippocampus of Angelman mice relative to WT controls (51 DEGs; false discovery rate (FDR) < 0.05) with only eight highly changed genes (log2-transformed fold expression < −0.75 or > 0.75; Extended Data Fig. 6c and Supplementary Table 5). By contrast, we found a much stronger signal in the cortex (1,717 DEGs, 777 up, 940 down; FDR < 0.05) with 189 highly changed genes (Fig. 4d,e and Supplementary Table 5). Given this more robust transcriptional response, we focused subsequent analyses on the cortex dataset.

We found only 22 DEGs (FDR < 0.05) in Het mice and 1,214 DEGs (623 up, 591 down; FDR < 0.05) in Rescue mice, 805 of which overlapped with Angelman mice (Fig. 5e). Gene Ontology (GO) analysis showed that downregulated genes implicated pathways related to mitochondrial function and protein synthesis, whereas upregulated genes were enriched for pathways related to neuronal growth and synaptic function (Fig. 4f,g). Notably, the significance of these changes was uniformly weakened in Rescue mice (Fig. 4f–i), strongly suggesting that they occurred as a direct result of ASNS overabundance. We specifically examined genes related to the ISR (GO: 0140467) and found only a weak enrichment, with just 7 of 51 genes in this set differentially expressed in Angelman mice (Extended Data Fig. 6d). We further compared our data with RNA-seq data from mice that lack Slc7a5, a gene encoding an essential amino acid transporter at the blood–brain barrier28. These mice show increased expression of genes encoding amino acid transporters (Slc7a1 and Slc7a3), transcription factors that are central to the ISR (Atf4 and Atf5) and transfer RNA (tRNA) synthases (Extended Data Fig. 6e). However, we did not detect changes in any of these genes in any of our genotypes, suggesting that these pathways are not activated in Angelman mice.

Values represent protein intensity relative to WT. a.u., arbitrary units. d,e, Polysome profiling using ribosomes isolated from P21 whole brains. Shown are tracks (d) and quantification (e) of polysome-to-monosome (P/M) ratios from P21 whole-brain lysates. Values represent P/M ratios relative to WT. f, Brain weights normalized by body weight in P21 mice. Solid symbols represent female mice and open symbols indicate male mice.
